*Xikai Sun, Kebin Liu, Haotian Wang, Li Liu, Xu Wang, Yunhao Liu*

Main category: cs.AI, cs.CV

Direction: 多模态理解与生成

Subtopic: 视频理解与时序推理

Importance: 值得关注 (76.9) - 未入选精读

TL;DR: 提出 Motion-as-Prompt (MaP)，一种轨迹引导的跨帧视觉提示框架，通过恢复密集点轨迹、选择运动信息丰富的帧并将轨迹直接标注到视觉输入上，使冻结的 MLLM 能观察到帧间隐藏的位移、方向变化与交互，从而提升运动推理能力。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: MLLM 通常通过稀疏均匀采样处理视频以控制视觉 token 和注意力成本，但这会丢弃采样帧之间的关键转换信息，限制模型对物体运动、碰撞和因果交互的推理能力。

Method: MaP 框架包含三个步骤：恢复密集点轨迹、选择运动信息丰富的帧、将连续采样帧之间累积的轨迹直接标注到视觉输入上，使隐藏的位移、方向变化和交互对冻结的 MLLM 可见，无需模型训练或架构修改。

Result: 在 CLEVRER 和 Something-Something-v2 上，MaP 一致提升平均运动推理准确率，对 GPT-5.5 分别带来 4.2% 和 8.9% 的提升，且不损害非运动理解能力。

Conclusion: MaP 提供了一种简单有效的运动中心视频推理增强方案，无需训练或架构改动即可提升 MLLM 的运动推理能力，同时保持非运动理解的鲁棒性。

Classification reason: 论文聚焦于视频中的运动推理（motion reasoning），通过轨迹引导的跨帧视觉提示增强 MLLM 对视频中物体运动、碰撞与因果交互的理解，属于视频理解与时序推理范畴，映射到 [CANONICAL] video_understanding_temporal。

Importance reason: Offers a training-free visual prompting method for motion reasoning in MLLMs, but is tangential to the user's core continuous-model and generation priorities. Signals: motion reasoning, visual prompting, MLLM, trajectory, cross-frame

Abstract: Motion-centric video reasoning is fundamental to interactive applications such as robotic manipulation and autonomous navigation. However, multimodal large language models (MLLMs) typically process videos through sparse uniform sampling to control visual-token and attention costs. This strategy may discard critical transitions between sampled frames, limiting reasoning about object movement, collisions, and causal interactions. To mitigate this issue, we propose Motion-as-Prompt (MaP), a track-guided cross-frame visual prompting framework. MaP recovers dense point trajectories, selects motion-informative frames, and marks the trajectories accumulated between consecutive sampled frames directly onto the visual inputs, making otherwise hidden displacement, direction changes, and interactions observable to frozen MLLMs. Experiments on CLEVRER and Something-Something-v2 show that MaP consistently improves average motion-reasoning accuracy, yielding gains of 4.2% and 8.9% for GPT-5.5, respectively. Notably, these improvements are obtained without degrading non-motion understanding, highlighting the robustness of MaP. These results demonstrate that MaP provides a simple and effective solution for enhancing motion-centric video reasoning without model training or architectural modification. Project page: .

</details>


### [87] [Glance, Scrutinize, and Think: Advancing Video Anomaly Detection from Training-Free to Agentic Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11260)
*Shibo Gao, Peipei Yang, Xu-Yao Zhang, Linlin Huang*

Main category: cs.AI, cs.CV

Direction: 多模态理解与生成

Subtopic: 视频理解与时序推理

Importance: 高优先级 (81.5) - 未入选精读

TL;DR: 本文提出从训练无关到智能体推理的视频异常检测方法。先提出 Glance then Scrutinize (GtS) 训练无关框架，利用静态与动态文本引导实现粗到细的异常定位与理解；再提出工具增强的智能体 VAD 方法，让多模态大语言模型学会调用视频裁剪工具、密集重采样帧并自我纠错，通过冷启动监督微调加强化学习训练。同时扩展 VAGU 基准为 VAGU-T（7567 个视频、21 类异常），并提出 JeAUG 联合评测指标。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 现有视频异常检测方法存在"何时-什么"分离问题：传统 DNN 方法能定位异常发生的时间但缺乏语义理解，而基于 LLM 的方法能解释发生了什么但缺乏精确时序定位。作者将其归因于缺乏统一的推理范式，受人类检查监控视频的"全局浏览-局部细查-迭代思考"方式启发，研究这一全局到局部范式。

Method: 提出两条路径：(1) GtS（Glance then Scrutinize）训练无关框架，使用静态与动态文本引导实现粗到细的异常定位与理解，平衡精度与速度；(2) 工具增强的智能体 VAD 方法，多模态大语言模型学习调用视频裁剪工具、检查密集重采样帧并自我纠正错误定位假设，通过冷启动监督微调加带联合答案-定位奖励的强化学习训练。同时扩展 VAGU 基准为 VAGU-T，并提出 JeAUG 联合评测指标。

Result: 实验表明 GtS 显著超越训练无关基线，而智能体模型在更高精度的同时推理速度更快。

Conclusion: 全局到局部的推理范式能有效弥合视频异常检测中时序定位与语义理解之间的鸿沟，训练无关方法提供高效基线，而工具增强的智能体方法通过自我纠错突破冻结外部模块的性能上限。

Classification reason: 论文聚焦视频异常检测（VAD），涉及时序定位、视频理解与多模态大语言模型推理，属于视频理解与时序推理方向，映射到 [CANONICAL] video_understanding_temporal。

Importance reason: Solid VAD work with agentic reasoning, but it is outside the user's core continuous-model, generation, and world-model priorities. Signals: video anomaly detection, agentic reasoning, multimodal LLM, training-free framework, benchmark extension

Abstract: Video Anomaly Detection (VAD) aims to identify anomalous events and localize their temporal intervals. Existing approaches exhibit a "when-what" dissociation: traditional DNN-based methods localize when anomalies occur but lack semantic understanding, whereas LLM-based methods explain what happens but neglect precise temporal grounding. We attribute this to the absence of a unified reasoning paradigm. Inspired by how humans inspect surveillance videos - glancing globally to form temporal hypotheses, scrutinizing suspicious segments, and thinking iteratively to correct errors - we study this global-to-local paradigm from two perspectives. We first propose Glance then Scrutinize (GtS), a training-free framework using static and dynamic textual guidance for coarse-to-fine anomaly grounding and understanding, balancing accuracy and speed. To break the ceiling imposed by frozen external modules, we further propose a tool-augmented agentic VAD method, where a multimodal large language model learns to invoke a video cropping tool, inspect densely resampled frames, and self-correct mislocalized hypotheses, via cold-start supervised fine-tuning followed by reinforcement learning with a joint answer-grounding reward. For training and evaluation, we extend our prior VAGU benchmark into VAGU-T (Video Anomaly Grounding, Understanding, and Thinking), comprising 7,567 real-world videos over 21 anomaly categories with human-validated grounding, explanations, QA pairs, and chain-of-thought tool-calling traces. We further introduce JeAUG, a metric jointly evaluating semantic interpretability and temporal precision. Experiments show that GtS substantially surpasses training-free baselines, while the agentic model delivers both higher accuracy and faster inference.

</details>
